# Sources

Every source the guide relies on, grouped by module. Official sources are preferred (see [STYLE-GUIDE.md §3](STYLE-GUIDE.md#3-accuracy-rules)). Each module unit adds its own section when it is written.

Observed for FIX-M03-04 (2026-10-06):
- Angular 22.2.1 `ngc` with `strictTemplates` on a throwaway component (deleted): a template reading a `private` member compiles (exit 0), as does `protected`; `{{ #c }}` fails with NG5002 "Private identifiers are not supported"; a missing member fails with TS2339 (control).
- Angular style guide, "Use protected on class members that are only used by a component's template": https://angular.dev/style-guide#use-protected-on-class-members-that-are-only-used-by-a-components-template
- Node 24: `new (class X extends null {})()` throws "Super constructor null of X is not a constructor"; a proxy `defineProperty` trap returning `false` makes `Object.defineProperty` throw in sloppy code, `Reflect.defineProperty` returns `false`; a sloppy `set` returning `false` does not throw; a derived constructor may run code before `super()` but `this` access throws `ReferenceError`; `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(new Error('x'))` is `['stack', 'message']`.

## Module 05 · Modules, memory, errors and modern features

Specifications and documentation (read 2026-10-06):
- ECMA-262 (current draft, § numbers as of 2026-10-06): Cyclic Module Records §16.2.1.6 https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-cyclic-module-records; InitializeEnvironment §16.2.1.7.3.1 https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-source-text-module-record-initialize-environment (import bindings, uninitialized lexical bindings, function declarations instantiated at link time); CreateImportBinding §9.1.1.5.5 https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-createimportbinding ("initialized immutable indirect binding"); InnerModuleEvaluation §16.2.1.6.1.3.1 https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-innermoduleevaluation; module environment GetThisBinding §9.1.1.5.4 https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-module-environment-records-getthisbinding.
- HTML Standard, module map (keyed by URL and module type; fetched, parsed and evaluated once per Document or worker; fetched with mode "cors"): https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#module-map
- HTML Standard, the `script` element (`async` and `defer` for module scripts; "The defer attribute has no effect on module scripts"): https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/scripting.html#attr-script-defer
- Node.js 24 ECMAScript modules docs and their history block (`doc/api/esm.md` on the v24.x branch: unflagged in v13.2.0 / v12.17.0): https://nodejs.org/api/esm.html
- MDN browser-compat-data `html/elements/script.json`, `type.module`: Chrome 61, Edge 79, Firefox 60, Safari 10.1: https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/html/elements/script.json
- JLS SE 21 §12.4.1 (when initialization occurs) and §12.4.2 (a recursive initialization request completes normally): https://docs.oracle.com/javase/specs/jls/se21/html/jls-12.html

- Section 2 (CommonJS value copy, the wrapper's five names and `this === module.exports`, cycle partial exports, ESM importing CommonJS with the namespace keys `default`/`greet`/`module.exports`/`version`, the computed-key `SyntaxError`, `require(esm)` namespace and `ERR_REQUIRE_ASYNC_MODULE`, `import()` identity, single evaluation and `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, `import()` from CommonJS): `section-2-commonjs-interop.test.ts` with `fixtures/section-2/`.
- Section 3 (`import.meta.url`/`filename`/`dirname`/`resolve`, JSON import with `with { type: 'json' }`, `ERR_IMPORT_ATTRIBUTE_MISSING` without it, no named exports from JSON, `assert` is a `SyntaxError`; `__dirname` in a module is `ReferenceError: __dirname is not defined`, run by hand): `section-3-meta-attributes.test.ts` with `fixtures/section-3/`.
- Section 4 (unreachable cycle collected; interval keeps a captured report alive until `clearInterval`; listener keeps a forgotten panel alive until removed; a kept `close` closure keeps it alive after removal), run with `node --expose-gc`, each fixture stable over 20 runs before the assertions were written: `section-4-gc-leaks.test.ts` with `fixtures/section-4/`.
- Section 5 (`WeakMap` has no `size`/`keys`/iterator; non-registered and well-known symbols accepted, `Symbol.for` key throws `TypeError: Invalid value used as weak map key`; `new WeakRef(42)` throws; a `WeakRef` target survives `gc()` in its own job and is collected in the next task; ephemeron key collected despite its value pointing back; `FinalizationRegistry` callback runs in a later task), GC fixtures stable over 20 runs with `--expose-gc`: `section-5-weak-refs.test.ts` with `fixtures/section-5/`.
- Section 6 (subclass `name`/field/`instanceof` and V8 stack header `ValidationError: email is required`; `cause` own property only when passed; Node prints `[cause]: Error: ECONNRESET`; `AggregateError` fields; `Error.isError` true and `instanceof` false for a `node:vm` error, the reverse for `Object.create(Error.prototype)`; a thrown string): `section-6-errors.test.ts` with `fixtures/section-6/print-cause.mjs`.

- Section 8 (the Node 24 column of the edition table: every ES2015–ES2025 row available; ES2026 `Error.isError`, `Array.fromAsync`, `JSON.rawJSON` present and `getOrInsert`, `Iterator.concat`, `Uint8Array.fromBase64`, `Math.sumPrecise` absent; of the ES2027-finished features `using`, `DisposableStack`, `Atomics.pause` present and Temporal, `Iterator.zip`, iterator `chunks`/`includes`/`join` absent), with a mutation check (a broken probe fails): `section-8-editions.test.ts`.

## Module 06 · TypeScript type system essentials

Specifications and documentation (read 2026-10-06):
- TypeScript 6.0 release notes (default changes: `strict`, `module` esnext, floating `target` es2025, `noUncheckedSideEffectImports`, `libReplacement`, `rootDir`, `types: []`; deprecations removed in 7.0; `ignoreDeprecations: "6.0"`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-6-0.html
- TypeScript 5.8 release notes, `--erasableSyntaxOnly` (motivated by Node's strip-only mode): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-8.html
- TSConfig reference, `isolatedModules` ("other transpilers only operate on a single file at a time"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/
- Announcing TypeScript 7.0 (native port "done as faithfully as possible", 8x–12x on full builds, adopts 6.0's defaults and makes its deprecations hard errors, no API in 7.0, Angular template checking stays on 6.0, `@typescript/typescript6`): https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/
- Node.js 24 TypeScript docs (`doc/api/typescript.md` on v24.x: type stripping enabled by default v23.6.0, no warning v24.3.0, stable v24.12.0; "no type checking is performed"; `enum`, namespaces with runtime code, parameter properties, import aliases need `--experimental-transform-types`; `tsconfig.json` ignored): https://nodejs.org/api/typescript.html
- TypeScript handbook, Type Compatibility ("based on structural subtyping"; private and protected members must originate from the same class): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/type-compatibility.html
- TypeScript handbook, Object Types (excess property checks on object literals; `readonly` not compared, "can also change via aliasing"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/objects.html
- TypeScript 2.4 release notes, weak type detection: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-2-4.html
- TypeScript handbook, Narrowing (control flow analysis; `typeof null` is `"object"`; truthiness narrowing): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/narrowing.html
- TypeScript 3.7 release notes, assertion functions: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-3-7.html
- TypeScript 5.4 release notes, preserved narrowing in closures following last assignments: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-4.html
- TypeScript 5.5 release notes, inferred type predicates: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-5.html
- TypeScript 3.9 release notes, intersections reduced by discriminant properties: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-3-9.html
- TypeScript handbook, More on Functions (`void` contextual return "can return any other value, but it will be ignored"; `object` vs `{}` vs `Object`, "Always use `object`!"; `unknown`; `never`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/functions.html
- TypeScript 4.4 release notes, `useUnknownInCatchVariables`: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-4-4.html
- TypeScript handbook, Enums (reverse mapping; string members get none; const enum members "inlined at use sites"; "you may not need an enum when an object with `as const` could suffice"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/enums.html
- TypeScript 3.4 release notes, const assertions: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-3-4.html
- TypeScript 4.9 release notes, the `satisfies` operator: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-4-9.html
- TypeScript 5.0 release notes, enum overhaul (out-of-domain literal is an error): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-0.html
- Package source, `@angular/core` 22.2.1 typings (`labs/angular/node_modules/@angular/core/types/`): `declare enum ChangeDetectionStrategy`, `declare enum ViewEncapsulation`, `type ResourceStatus = 'idle' | 'error' | 'loading' | 'reloading' | 'resolved' | 'local'`.
- Angular style guide (angular.dev/style-guide, read 2026-10-06): no mention of enums: https://angular.dev/style-guide
- TypeScript handbook, Modules: Choosing compiler options (bundler: `"moduleResolution": "bundler"`; Node: `"module": "nodenext"`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/modules/guides/choosing-compiler-options.html
- TypeScript handbook, Choosing compiler options, library section (`"module": "node18"`, `declaration: true`, lowest `target`; "When a codebase is compatible with Node.js's module system, it almost always works in bundlers as well"; `verbatimModuleSyntax` "protects against a few module-related pitfalls"), read 2026-10-06: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/modules/guides/choosing-compiler-options.html
- Angular, Making HTTP requests ("The generic type of request methods is a type **assertion** … `HttpClient` does not verify that the actual return data matches this type"), read 2026-10-06: https://angular.dev/guide/http/making-requests
- Node.js 24 ESM docs, Mandatory file extensions ("A file extension must be provided when using the `import` keyword to resolve relative or absolute specifiers"), read 2026-10-06: https://nodejs.org/api/esm.html#mandatory-file-extensions
- TSConfig reference, `strict` ("equivalent to enabling all of the strict mode family options") — same page as `isolatedModules` above. TypeScript 5.4 release notes (already listed): `--module preserve` implies `moduleResolution: bundler`, `esModuleInterop`, `resolveJsonModule`.
- Package source, typescript 6.0.3 `ts.optionDeclarations` (`strictFlag` entries) read in the lab; `labs/angular/tsconfig.json` (`"module": "preserve"`).
- TypeScript handbook, Declaration Files: Consumption (`@types` not needed when the package includes declarations): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/declaration-files/consumption.html
- TypeScript handbook, Declaration Merging (module augmentation and its two limitations; global augmentation): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/declaration-merging.html
- TSConfig reference, `skipLibCheck` ("Skip type checking of declaration files") — same page as above.
- Package source in `labs/ts-js/node_modules`: `picomatch` 4.0.7 (no `types`, no `@types`), `chai` (no `types`/`typings`; `@types/chai` 5.2.3 installed), `vitest` (`exports['.'].import.types` = `./dist/index.d.ts`).
- npm registry, read 2026-10-06: `typescript` dist-tags `latest` 7.0.2, `next` 7.1.0-dev; `@angular/compiler-cli@22.2.1` peer `typescript >=6.0 <6.1` (also in VERSIONS.md).

- TypeScript handbook, Modules Reference (`node16`/`node18` are fixed-version modes; "the `nodenext` mode changes with the latest stable versions of Node.js"), read 2026-10-06: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/modules/reference.html
- TypeScript 5.6 release notes (`--strictBuiltinIteratorReturn`), read 2026-10-06: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-6.html
- Re-read 2026-10-06 for FIX-M06: TSConfig reference `typeRoots` ("By default all visible `@types` packages are included in your compilation"; `strictBuiltinIteratorReturn` "Released: 5.6"); TypeScript 6.0 notes (`"types": ["*"]` "will restore the 5.9 behavior"; `rootDir` "./dist/src/index.js"; `noUncheckedSideEffectImports` default true); Node.js 24 TypeScript history (v23.6.0 enabled by default, v24.12.0 stable); Announcing TypeScript 7.0 ("Practically any TypeScript code that compiles cleanly with TypeScript 6.0 … should compile identically in TypeScript 7.0.").

- Exercise 06.3 (`as const satisfies` flag table: misspelled name TS2345, misspelled property TS2561 plus TS2322 at the widening, `FlagName` equals the three-name union both ways, literals `25`/`false` kept, exact `transpileModule` emit; by hand: `Record<FlagName, FlagConfig>` missing entry TS2741, extra TS2353, widened `enabled` TS2322): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/06-ts-type-system-essentials/feature-flags.test.ts`
- Exercise 06.4 (the legacy cart compiles with `strict: false` and gives TS7006 ×3, TS2538, TS2532, TS2322, TS2375 under the lab settings plus `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`; the strict rewrite has none; one run-time test body passes against both, the legacy one transpiled and evaluated): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/06-ts-type-system-essentials/cart.test.ts`
- Section 3 (FIX-M06): `instanceof` with an interface is TS2693, with a class it compiles; an assignment narrows a declared union to the assigned type and branches merge back: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/06-ts-type-system-essentials/section-3-narrowing.test.ts`
- Section 6 (FIX-M06): `let a = 'x'` widens to `string` (TS2322 to `'x'`), `const` keeps the literal; TS2748 is reported at the use of the ambient const enum: `section-6-enums-satisfies.test.ts`

## Module 07 · Advanced types, decorators and runtime safety

Specifications and documentation (read 2026-10-06):
- TypeScript handbook, More on Functions, "Guidelines for Writing Good Generic Functions" ("If a type parameter only appears in one location, strongly reconsider if you actually need it"; "type parameters are for relating the types of multiple values"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/functions.html
- TypeScript 5.0 release notes, `const` type parameters ("cause `const`-like inference to be the default"; a mutable constraint "might give surprising results"; recommends `readonly string[]`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-0.html
- TypeScript 5.4 release notes, `NoInfer` ("a signal to TypeScript not to dig in and match against the inner types to find candidates for type inference"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-4.html

- TypeScript 4.1 release notes (template literal types; key remapping with `as`; intrinsic `Uppercase`/`Lowercase`/`Capitalize`/`Uncapitalize`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-4-1.html
- TypeScript handbook, Mapped Types ("You can remove or add these modifiers by prefixing with `-` or `+`"; filtering keys by producing `never`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/mapped-types.html
- TypeScript handbook, Keyof Type Operator (`string | number` "because JavaScript object keys are always coerced to a string"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/keyof-types.html
- TypeScript handbook, Conditional Types ("they become *distributive* when given a union type"; "surround each side of the `extends` keyword with square brackets"; `infer`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/conditional-types.html
- TypeScript 4.5 release notes, tail-recursion elimination on conditional types ("As long as one branch of a conditional type is simply another conditional type …"; heuristics "much more generous"; no numeric limit stated) and `Awaited`: https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-4-5.html
- TypeScript 2.6 release notes, strict function types ("checked *contravariantly* instead of *bivariantly*"; "except those originating in method or constructor declarations … (such as `Array<T>`) continue to mostly relate covariantly"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-2-6.html
- TypeScript 4.7 release notes, optional variance annotations (`out`/`in`/`in out`; checked by the compiler; recommended for library authors with deeply recursive types, "wins in both accuracy and type-checking speed"): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-4-7.html
- Java Language Specification SE 21, §10.5 Array Store Exception ("an assignment to a component of the array is checked at run time"), for the backend comparison: https://docs.oracle.com/javase/specs/jls/se21/html/jls-10.html
- TypeScript 5.0 release notes, decorators ("`--experimentalDecorators` will continue to exist for the foreseeable future"; "not compatible with `--emitDecoratorMetadata`, and it does not allow decorating parameters"; `accessor`, `context.addInitializer`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-0.html
- TypeScript 5.2 release notes, decorator metadata ("an upcoming ECMAScript feature"; needs a `Symbol.metadata` polyfill): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-2.html
- TC39 proposal-decorators README ("**Stage**: 2.7"; an older passage in the same README says "decorators have reached stage 3", which module 05 line 517 now cites as having moved back; "Decorators are _functions_ called on classes, class elements, or other JavaScript syntax forms during definition"): https://github.com/tc39/proposal-decorators
- TC39 proposals README, "Stage 2.7" table lists Decorators and Decorator Metadata (read 2026-10-06): https://github.com/tc39/proposals
- `@schematics/angular` 22.2.1 as installed in `labs/angular`, `workspace/files/tsconfig.json.template` line 13: `"experimentalDecorators": true`.
- Zod 4.6.5 as installed in `labs/ts-js` (`npm ls zod`): `v4/classic/schemas.d.ts` lines 530–532 (`object` → `$strip`, `strictObject` → `$strict`, `looseObject` → `$loose`), `.brand<T, Dir = "out">`; `v4/core/core.d.ts` (`export type { output as infer }`, `$brand`, `$ZodBranded`).
- Zod API docs ("By default, unrecognized keys are *stripped* from the parsed result"; strict and loose objects; read 2026-10-06; the page also documents `.validate()`, which 4.6.5's `.d.ts` does not have, so the module does not use it): https://zod.dev/api
- Zod basics docs (`.parse()` "will throw a `ZodError` instance"; `.safeParse()` result "is a discriminated union"; `z.infer`, `z.input`/`z.output` after `.transform()`): https://zod.dev/basics
- `@angular/common` 22.2.1 `types/http.d.ts` (`get<T>(url, …)` overloads; `T` is a type argument only).
- Angular API, `InjectionToken` ("`InjectionToken` is parameterized on `T` which is the type of object which will be returned by the `Injector`"): https://angular.dev/api/core/InjectionToken
- Angular typed forms guide ("As of Angular 14, reactive forms are strictly typed by default"; `null` because "the control can become `null` at any time, by calling reset"; `nonNullable`; `value` is `Partial<…>` with disabled controls; `getRawValue()`; `Untyped` symbols for migration): https://angular.dev/guide/forms/typed-forms
- Angular inputs guide (an unset input "Produces an `InputSignal<number | undefined>`"; "Required inputs do not automatically include `undefined`"; input transforms): https://angular.dev/guide/components/inputs
- Angular template type checking (`strictContextGenerics`: "Whether the type parameters of generic components will be inferred correctly"): https://angular.dev/tools/cli/template-typecheck
- `@angular/router` 22.2.1 `types/_router_module-chunk.d.ts`: `type ResolveFn<T> = (route, state) => MaybeAsync<T | RedirectCommand>`, `type MaybeAsync<T> = T | Observable<T> | Promise<T>`.
- TypeScript 4.8 release notes (`NonNullable<T>` became `T & {}`; old form `T extends null | undefined ? never : T`): https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-4-8.html
- TC39 process document (Stage 2.7: "The proposal is approved in principle and undergoing validation"): https://tc39.es/process-document/
- Zod 4 changelog (`z.strictObject()`/`z.looseObject()` recommended; `.strict()`/`.passthrough()` legacy, "will not be removed"; `.strip()` "was never particularly useful, as it was the default behavior of `z.object()`"): https://zod.dev/v4/changelog
- `@angular/core` 22.2.1 `fesm2022/_debug_node-chunk.mjs` line 979 (`PropDecorator`: `if (target === undefined) throw new Error('Standard Angular field decorators are not supported in JIT mode.')`), read from `labs/angular/node_modules`.
- `@angular/router` 22.2.1 `types/_router_module-chunk.d.ts`: `type Data = { [key: string | symbol]: any }` (line 2205), `Route.data?: Data`, `ActivatedRoute.data: Observable<Data>`, `ActivatedRouteSnapshot.data: Data`; `ResolveFn`/`RedirectCommand` carry a plain `@publicApi` tag, so no status marker.
- Angular template type checking, also: `strictContextGenerics` ("If disabled, any type parameters will be `any`"), enabled by `strictTemplates`; `$any()` "treats it as a cast to the `any` type" (read 2026-10-06; see the template type checking entry above).

Observed by running (typescript 6.0.3 compiler API through `labs/ts-js/src/modules/06-ts-type-system-essentials/typecheck.ts`):
- Section 1 (`any` vs `T` element type TS2322; constraint TS2345; `K extends keyof T` misspelled key TS2345 and `T[K][]` = `number[]`; default `unknown` TS18046; first-candidate inference TS2345; callback return inferred `string[]`; return-only parameter inferred from the annotation (compiles) and `unknown` without one; `const T` → `readonly ["a", "b"]`, under `extends string[]` → `["a", "b"]` (differs from the 5.0 notes' fallback), under `extends readonly string[]` → `readonly ["a", "b"]`; `NoInfer` TS2345 vs widened `"sm" | "md" | "lg"`; a `never` probe written directly on a call changes inference, so the test reads types through a `const`): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-1-generics.test.ts`
- Section 2 (exact-equality checks with an `Equal<A, B>` type that distinguishes `readonly`: `keyof User`, `User['age']`, union index access; `keyof Record<string, number>` = `string` vs `keyof { [key: string]: number }` = `string | number`; `(typeof ROLES)[number]`; `-readonly`, `-?`, `readonly` mappings; homomorphic copy equals `User`; a mapping over an explicit key union turns optional `email` into `string | undefined`; `Getters` via `as` + `Capitalize`; `never` filtering drops methods; template literal cross product and TS2322): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-2-mapped-types.test.ts`
- Section 3 (distribution `'yes' | 'no'` vs bracketed `'no'`; `ToArray<string | number>`; `never` in → `never`, bracketed → `'yes'`; `infer` from arrays, promises, functions, template literals (`RouteParams` → `'userId' | 'postId'`); naive `DeepReadonly`: `push` TS2339, nested write TS2540, method call TS2349, fixed by a function branch; recursive `Json` rejects a function TS2322; TS2589 at `Build<1500>` (tail, `Build<999>` fine) and at `Repeat<100>` (non-tail, `Repeat<40>` fine)): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-3-conditional-types.test.ts`
- Section 4 (`lib.es5.d.ts` in typescript 6.0.3 read for `Partial`, `Pick`, `Omit`, `Exclude`, `ReturnType`, `NonNullable`, `Awaited` text and the five `= intrinsic` declarations (`Uppercase`, `Lowercase`, `Capitalize`, `Uncapitalize`, `NoInfer`; elsewhere only `lib.es2015.iterable.d.ts` declares one, `BuiltinIteratorReturn`); hand-written `Partial`/`Pick`/`Omit`/`ReturnType`/simplified `Awaited` equal the built-ins; `Pick` keeps `readonly` and `?`; derived DTOs; `Omit<User, 'nmae'>` compiles and equals `User`, `Pick<User, 'nmae'>` TS2344; `Omit` over a union keeps only `kind`, a distributive wrapper keeps both members): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-4-utility-types.test.ts`
- Section 5 (property-syntax `Dog` handler TS2322, accepted with `strictFunctionTypes: false`; `Animal` handler fits a `Dog` slot; method-syntax handler compiles under `strict` and throws `TypeError: dog.bark is not a function` when run; `Dog[]` → `Animal[]` compiles and a pushed animal makes `bark()` throw; `in`/`out`: `BadConsumer<in T> { get(): T }` and `out T` with a property `set` TS2636, `out T` with a `set` *method* passes; `Producer<Animal>` → `Producer<Dog>` TS2322): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-5-variance.test.ts`
- Section 6 (plain string aliases: swapped IDs compile; `unique symbol` brands: swapped and raw-string arguments TS2345, raw literal as `UserId` TS2322; `Cents` assignable to `number`, `price + tax` compiles as `number`, `const total: Cents = price + tax` TS2322; at run time `addCents(cents(1999), cents(1))` is `2000`, a bare number with no own keys; `cents(19.99)` throws `RangeError`): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-6-brands.test.ts`
- Section 7 (standard decorators without `experimentalDecorators`: the fixture type-checks; `transpileModule` at ES2022 emits `__esDecorate`/`__runInitializers` and no `@trace`; run order: evaluate outer, evaluate inner, apply inner, apply outer, class defined, initializers inner then outer on construction, `accessor` `init`, `set`, calls outer → inner → body; a parameter decorator TS1206, none with `experimentalDecorators: true`; Vitest does not transform standard decorators, so the test runs the transpiled output): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-7-decorators.test.ts` with `fixtures/section-7/decorated-cart.ts`
- Section 7, Angular (re-run 2026-10-06, `npx ngc -p tsconfig.app.json --rootDir src --outDir <tmp>` in `labs/angular`, output deleted): `App` has static `ɵfac` and `ɵcmp = i0.ɵɵdefineComponent(…)`, no `__decorate` and no `@Component`, and the arguments only in an `ngDevMode`-guarded `ɵsetClassMetadata`; with `experimentalDecorators: false` the `App` output is unchanged and `@Inject` on a constructor parameter is TS1206. At run time `ɵcmp` is an object and `ɵfac` a function, and a component using `inject()` renders: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/decorators.spec.ts` (`ng test`)
- Section 8 (zod 4.6.5: `z.infer` of a `User` schema exactly equals `{ id: string; age: number; email?: string | undefined; nickname: string | null }`, an extra key in a typed literal TS2353; `parse` strips an unknown `role`; a wrong type throws `ZodError` with `invalid_type`, path `['id']`, "Invalid input: expected string, received number"; `safeParse` of `age: 3.5` gives "Invalid input: expected int, received number" and `{ success: true, data }` on valid input; `strictObject` → `unrecognized_keys` `Unrecognized key: "role"`; `looseObject` keeps the key; a transform: `z.input` = `string`, `z.output` = `z.infer` = `number`; `.brand<'Cents'>()`: parsed value accepted, raw `1999` TS2345, value a plain number): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/section-8-zod.test.ts`
- Section 9, Angular 22.2.1 (`npx tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json --noEmit` for the exact-equality and `@ts-expect-error` lines, `ng test` for the rest): `inject` of a typed token is `ApiConfig`, of an untyped one `unknown`; `new FormControl('…')` is `FormControl<string | null>` and `reset()` gives `null`, `nonNullable` gives `FormControl<string>` and resets to the initial value; a `nonNullable.group` with a disabled `plan`: `value` typed `Partial<{ email: string; plan: string }>` and equal to `{ email }`, `getRawValue()` full, assigning `value` to the full type TS2322; `input<number>()` `InputSignal<number | undefined>`, `input.required<string>()` `InputSignal<string>`, `booleanAttribute` transform `InputSignalWithTransform<boolean, unknown>` and `''` reads `true`; `ResolveFn<User>` returning `{ id }` TS2322; generic `Picker<T>` in a host emits the clicked `User`. Probe (spec edited, run, restored): a `label` of `(count: number) => string` next to `[items]="users"` fails `ng test`'s build with `TS2322: Type 'User[]' is not assignable to type 'number[]'` and `Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'User'`. Also observed: `ng test` compiles specs with the Angular compiler (`NG8110` for `input()` outside a component): `labs/angular/src/app/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/typing-patterns.spec.ts`
- Q07.01–Q07.07 (fixtures `q07-02`, `q07-03`, `q07-06`, `q07-07`: `const T` → `readonly ["sm", "md"]`, plain `T extends readonly string[]` → `string[]`, two-site inference → `"sm" | "md" | "lg"`, `NoInfer` TS2345 on line 9; `keyof`, `-readonly`/`-?` (removes `undefined`), explicit-key mapping (`readonly` and `?` lost), `as`-remapped mapping keeps `readonly` and `?`, template cross product; distribution results incl. `IsString<never>` = `never`, `Wrapped<never>` = `'yes'`, `Exclude` → `1`; naive `DeepReadonly`: TS2339, TS2540, TS2349 on lines 9–11, function branch removes TS2349; `DeepReadonly<() => number>` = `{}`, `DeepReadonly<number[]>` = `readonly number[]`, fixed version leaves `Map#set` callable; `any` return compiles into `number`, `T | undefined` TS2322; the exact-equality check reports a `readonly` near miss): `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/generics-and-computed-types.test.ts`
- Q07.08–Q07.14 (`Exclude`/`Extract` on a role union, `Pick`/`Omit` on an object, `Exclude<User, 'id'>` = `User`; fixture `q07-11`: a method-syntax `handle(event: AppEvent)` accepts a `PaymentEvent` handler under `strict`, and running the transpiled file throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toFixed')`; property syntax → TS2322 on line 17; the narrowing handler compiles and records `['5.00']`; a constructor parameter decorator TS1206 by default, none with `experimentalDecorators`): `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/utility-variance-brands-decorators.test.ts`
- Q07.15–Q07.17 (fixture `q07-15` under standard decorators: type-checks, and its lowered output logs `evaluate A, evaluate B, apply B, apply A, defined, init B, init A, call A, call B, body`; under `experimentalDecorators` the same file gives TS1241 and TS1270 on lines 19 and 20; fixture `q07-17` with zod 4.6.5: `{"id":"u-1","age":30}`, `Invalid input: expected int, received number`, `unrecognized_keys`): `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/decorators-zod-angular.test.ts`
- Exercise 07.1 (`createEventBus`: registration order; self-unsubscribing handler during `emit`; consumer of the real file: unknown names TS2345, wrong payloads TS2322/TS2353/TS2345, `void` payload emitted bare, missing or extra argument TS2554, handler receives `undefined`; an interface event map fails `Record<string, unknown>` with TS2344): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/07-ts-advanced-types-and-decorators/event-bus.test.ts`

## Module 08 · Browser rendering, the DOM and events

Specifications and documentation (read 2026-10-06):
- jsdom (the labs' DOM; 30.1.1 in `labs/ts-js` and `labs/angular`): https://github.com/jsdom/jsdom
- web.dev, render-blocking CSS ("By default, CSS is treated as a render blocking resource"; media queries make some CSS non-blocking): https://web.dev/articles/critical-rendering-path/render-blocking-css
- web.dev, adding interactivity with JavaScript ("By default, JavaScript execution is 'parser blocking'"; "JavaScript execution blocks on the CSSOM"): https://web.dev/articles/critical-rendering-path/adding-interactivity-with-javascript
- web.dev, preload scanner ("a secondary HTML parser that scans ahead of the primary one if it's blocked"): https://web.dev/articles/preload-scanner
- web.dev, rendering performance (the pixel pipeline: JavaScript, style, layout, paint, composite; layers): https://web.dev/articles/rendering-performance
- MDN, critical rendering path (DOM, CSSOM, render tree, layout, paint; `display: none` excluded from the render tree): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Performance/Guides/Critical_rendering_path
- MDN, `<script>` (`defer` runs "after the document has been parsed, but before firing `DOMContentLoaded`", in order; classic `async` "evaluated as soon as it is available"; "The defer attribute has no effect on module scripts — they defer by default"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/script

- web.dev, avoid large, complex layouts and layout thrashing (JavaScript, then style, then layout; reading `offsetHeight` after a class change makes the browser "first apply the style change … and then run layout"; "read style values then make style changes"; old layout values from the previous frame are available to reads before writes): https://web.dev/articles/avoid-large-complex-layouts-and-layout-thrashing
- web.dev, stick to compositor-only properties and manage layer count ("Stick to transform and opacity changes for your animations"; the element "should be on its own compositor layer"; "every layer you create requires memory and management"): https://web.dev/articles/stick-to-compositor-only-properties-and-manage-layer-count
- web.dev, rendering performance, also for §2 (layout changes make the browser "reflow" the page; for a paint-only property "the layout step is not necessary"): https://web.dev/articles/rendering-performance
- MDN, `will-change` ("a last resort to try to deal with existing performance problems"; "Excessive use of will-change will result in excessive memory use"; Baseline widely available): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Properties/will-change
- MDN, `content-visibility` (`auto` skips rendering work for off-screen content; example with `contain-intrinsic-size: auto 500px`; Baseline 2024 newly available, since September 2024): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Properties/content-visibility
- web.dev, content-visibility (with size containment the element "will lay out as if it was empty", 0 height, so the scrollbar shifts; `contain-intrinsic-size` "acts as a placeholder size"; off-screen `auto` content "remains available in the document object model and therefore, the accessibility tree" and "can be searched for on the page"). Discrepancy: this page says Baseline newly available on Sept 15, 2025; the guide follows MDN (2024): https://web.dev/articles/content-visibility
- DOM Standard, mutation observers (one "mutation observer microtask" queued per agent; records delivered together): https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#mutation-observers
- MDN, `DocumentFragment` (appending "moves the fragment's nodes into the DOM, leaving behind an empty DocumentFragment"; "The performance benefit of DocumentFragment is often overstated"; slower than appending in a loop in some engines): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/DocumentFragment
- MDN, `MutationObserver.takeRecords()` (returns pending records, "leaving the mutation queue empty"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/MutationObserver/takeRecords
- MDN, Intersection Observer API (asynchronous observation; the callback "is executed on the main thread"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Intersection_Observer_API
- MDN, `IntersectionObserver()` constructor (defaults: "the document's viewport as the root, with no margin, and a 0% threshold"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/IntersectionObserver/IntersectionObserver
- MDN, `ResizeObserver` (loop limit; error message "ResizeObserver loop completed with undelivered notifications"; Baseline widely available): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/ResizeObserver
- MDN, `ResizeObserver.observe()` (`box`: `content-box` by default, `border-box`, `device-pixel-content-box`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/ResizeObserver/observe
- MDN, `HTMLElement.dataset` (a map of strings; set values are converted to strings): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLElement/dataset
- DOM Standard, events (`stopImmediatePropagation()` sets "the stop propagation flag and … the stop immediate propagation flag"; the default passive value: `touchstart`, `touchmove`, `wheel`, `mousewheel` on a Window, a document, its document element or its body element): https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#dom-event-stopimmediatepropagation, https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#default-passive-value
- MDN, `addEventListener()` (options `capture`, `once`, `passive`, `signal`; a passive listener "cannot cancel the event, so the browser does not need to wait for it to finish before scrolling"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/EventTarget/addEventListener
- HTML Standard, custom elements (constructor requirements: "The element must not gain any attributes or children"): https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/custom-elements.html#custom-element-conformance
- MDN, using custom elements (name must "start with a lowercase letter, contain a hyphen"; lifecycle callbacks; `observedAttributes`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_components/Using_custom_elements
- MDN, `attachShadow()` (closed: `shadowRoot` "is set to null"; on an element with a declarative shadow root of the same mode, "the ShadowRoot that was already present will be cleared and returned"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/attachShadow
- MDN, using shadow DOM (closed mode is not "a strong security mechanism", e.g. browser extensions; the declarative option for server-side rendered UIs): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_components/Using_shadow_DOM
- MDN, `::part` (styling a shadow tree from outside; `exportparts`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Selectors/::part
- MDN, `<template>` (with `shadowrootmode` "the HTML parser will immediately generate a shadow DOM"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/template
- MDN, `HTMLTemplateElement.shadowRootMode` (Baseline widely available): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLTemplateElement/shadowRootMode
- MDN, `Event.composed` (UA-dispatched UI events such as click are composed): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Event/composed
- `@angular/core` 22.2.1 typings, `ViewEncapsulation` (`Emulated` adds an attribute to the host and to the component's selectors, the default; `ShadowDom` uses a native shadow root; `ExperimentalIsolatedShadowDom` experimental since 21.0): `labs/angular/node_modules/@angular/core/types/_debug_node-chunk.d.ts`
- web.dev, Web Vitals (75th percentile of page loads, segmented by mobile and desktop; the `web-vitals` library and its `onCLS`/`onINP`/`onLCP` example; CrUX powers Search Console's Core Web Vitals report): https://web.dev/articles/vitals
- web.dev, defining the Core Web Vitals thresholds (table: LCP ≤2500 ms / >4000 ms, INP ≤200 ms / >500 ms, CLS ≤0.1 / >0.25, 75th percentile): https://web.dev/articles/defining-core-web-vitals-thresholds
- web.dev, LCP ("the render time of the largest image, text block, or video visible in the viewport"): https://web.dev/articles/lcp
- web.dev, INP ("all click, tap, and keyboard interactions"; successor to FID, which "only measured the input delay of the first interaction"; "some lab tools won't report a page's INP because they only observe the loading of a page", TBT a proxy, "not a substitute"): https://web.dev/articles/inp
- web.dev, CLS ("the largest burst of layout shift scores for every unexpected layout shift"; poor above 0.25): https://web.dev/articles/cls
- web.dev, optimize CLS ("Images without dimensions": set `width`/`height` or `aspect-ratio`; content inserted above existing content): https://web.dev/articles/optimize-cls
- web.dev blog, INP is a stable Core Web Vital, replacing FID (post dated 2024-03-12; FID support in Chrome tools until September 9, 2024): https://web.dev/blog/inp-cwv-launch
- MDN, `popstate` ("just calling history.pushState() or history.replaceState() won't trigger a popstate event"; fired on back/forward or `history.back()`/`forward()`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/popstate_event
- HTML Standard, history push/replace state steps (`StructuredSerializeForStorage(data)`, "Rethrow any exceptions"): https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/nav-history-apis.html#shared-history-push/replace-state-steps
- MDN, Navigation API (Baseline 2026 newly available, since January 2026; successor to the History API for SPAs; `currentEntry`, `entries()`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Navigation_API
- MDN, `navigate` event ("fired when any type of navigation is initiated"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Navigation/navigate_event
- MDN, `NavigateEvent.canIntercept` (cross-origin navigations cannot be intercepted) and `NavigateEvent` (`hashChange`, `downloadRequest`, `destination`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/NavigateEvent/canIntercept, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/NavigateEvent
- `@angular/common` 22.2.1 `fesm2022/_platform_location-chunk.mjs` (browser `PlatformLocation` calls `history.pushState` and listens to `popstate`) and `@angular/router` 22.2.1 `types/router.d.ts` (`withExperimentalPlatformNavigation()`, "_highly_ experimental", `@experimental 21.1`), in `labs/angular/node_modules`
- DOM Standard, dispatch (the path is walked in reverse invoking "capturing" listeners, with the target at `AT_TARGET`, then forward invoking "bubbling" listeners): https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-event-dispatch

- Section 3 (jsdom 30.1.1: an appended fragment is left empty; `template.content` is a fragment; `MutationObserver` delivers 3 records in one callback after the synchronous code; `takeRecords()` empties the queue so the callback never runs; `dataset` entries are strings and `Number('')` is `0`, `valueAsNumber` `NaN`; jsdom has no `IntersectionObserver`/`ResizeObserver`): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/08-browser-rendering-dom-events/section-3-dom-observers.test.ts`
- Section 4 (jsdom 30.1.1: capture → target → bubble order with `eventPhase` 1/2/3; `stopPropagation` lets same-element listeners run, `stopImmediatePropagation` does not; `preventDefault` needs `cancelable` and does not stop propagation; passive `preventDefault` ignored; jsdom applies the default-passive rule to `wheel` on window, `passive: false` opts out, a button listener is not passive; `once` and `signal`; delegation via `closest`; `focus` does not bubble, `focusin` does; synchronous `dispatchEvent` with `CustomEvent.detail`): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/08-browser-rendering-dom-events/section-4-events.test.ts`
- Section 5 (jsdom 30.1.1: lifecycle order constructor → observed attribute change (`null -> 1`, unobserved ignored) → connected → disconnected; a name without a hyphen throws `DOMException`; an element parsed before `define()` is upgraded; closed root gives `shadowRoot === null`; named and default slot assignment, `slotchange` after a microtask; a composed event is retargeted to the host, a non-composed one stops at the shadow root): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/08-browser-rendering-dom-events/section-5-web-components.test.ts`
- Section 7 (jsdom 30.1.1 at Vitest's `http://localhost:3000/`: `pushState` changes the URL and fires no `popstate`; `back()` returns with the URL unchanged, then `popstate` brings the earlier entry's state; jsdom does not structured-clone state (a function is kept, where the HTML Standard throws); no `navigation` global): `labs/ts-js/src/modules/08-browser-rendering-dom-events/section-7-history.test.ts`
- Q08.06 (jsdom 30.1.1: `sync done`, `observer: 2 records`, `promise`): `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/08-browser-rendering-dom-events/rendering-and-dom.test.ts`
- Q08.08, Q08.09, Q08.12 and follow-ups (jsdom 30.1.1: at the target the capture listener runs before the bubble one; `A`, `B`, `C`, `false true`, and `A`, `B`, `false true` with `stopImmediatePropagation`; without `cancelable` `true false`; `root sees BUTTON` ×2 then `document sees DIV`; a composed non-bubbling event reaches the host listener only): `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/08-browser-rendering-dom-events/events-and-shadow.test.ts`
- HTML Standard, update document for history step application (fires `popstate` when the document's entry changed and the document is not new, then queues `hashchange` if the fragment differs; "navigate to a fragment" calls it), read 2026-10-06: https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/browsing-the-web.html#update-document-for-history-step-application
- MDN, Baseline compatibility (newly available: latest stable version of each core browser; widely available: at least 2.5 years later), read 2026-10-06: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/Baseline/Compatibility
- MDN, `mousewheel` event (Deprecated, Non-standard; use `wheel`), read 2026-10-06: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/mousewheel_event
- MDN, `Event.composedPath()` (excludes nodes in closed shadow roots), read 2026-10-06: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Event/composedPath
- MDN, `MutationEvent` (Deprecated; "selected for removal ... as part of Interop 2025"; use `MutationObserver`), read 2026-10-06: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/MutationEvent
- mdn/content issue 3936 (secondary source, user report: from Chromium 89.0.4359.0 capture listeners run before non-capture ones at the target; before, registration order), read 2026-10-06: https://github.com/mdn/content/issues/3936
- web-vitals README (callbacks may run more than once; CLS and INP reported when `visibilityState` becomes hidden; `reportAllChanges`; `sendBeacon` example), read 2026-10-06: https://github.com/GoogleChrome/web-vitals
- TC39 finished proposals (nullish coalescing ES2020; `Promise.any`/`AggregateError` ES2021; class fields ES2022), read 2026-10-06: https://github.com/tc39/proposals/blob/main/finished-proposals.md
- Also read 2026-10-06 for FIX-M08: MDN `popstate` (hashchange follows popstate when fragments differ); MDN `<template>` (non-standard `shadowroot` attribute "supported in Chrome 90-110", replaced by `shadowrootmode`; the Baseline row is JS-rendered, so the marker keeps the `shadowRootMode` page); web.dev INP (selection: worst interaction, one highest ignored per 50; phases input delay, processing duration, presentation delay); web.dev CLS (session window "less than 1-second in between each shift and a maximum of 5 seconds"); web.dev LCP lists no sub-parts, so none are taught.

## Module 17 · Signals

Primary (read directly, Angular 22.2.1 as installed in `labs/angular`):
- `@angular/core/types/core.d.ts`, `_api-chunk.d.ts`, `_debug_node-chunk.d.ts`, `rxjs-interop.d.ts`: signatures and docs for `signal`, `computed`, `linkedSignal`, `effect`, `afterRenderEffect`, `resource`, `ResourceStatus`, `ResourceParamsStatus`, `debounced` (experimental), `input`, `model`, `output`, `toSignal`, `toObservable`, `rxResource`.
- `@angular/core/fesm2022/_effect-chunk.mjs`: the reactive graph implementation (`producerAccessed`, `producerUpdateValueVersion`, `consumerPollProducersForChange`, `signalSetFn`, the `COMPUTED_NODE` and `ERRORED` handling, `runEffect`).
- `@angular/core/fesm2022/_resource-chunk.mjs`: `OutputEmitterRef` destroy behavior (NG0953). `_debug_node-chunk.mjs`: required query error (NG0951). `core.mjs`: required input and model errors.
- `@angular/core/schematics/collection.json`: migration schematic names.
- `@angular-eslint/eslint-plugin` 22.5.0 package contents (`npm pack @angular-eslint/eslint-plugin@22.5.0`, then the rule names under `dist/`): `prefer-signals`, `prefer-signal-model`, `no-uncalled-signals`, `reactive-context-must-read-signal`. Rule docs: https://github.com/angular-eslint/angular-eslint/tree/main/packages/eslint-plugin/docs/rules
- `@angular/core` 22.0.0 and 22.1.0 typings (`npm pack`), used to date `ResourceParamsContext.chain` (present since 22.0.0).
- Angular changelog 20.0.0 breaking changes: `TestBed.flushEffects()` removed; `TestBed.tick()` introduced.

Documentation and changelogs:
- Angular effects guide: https://angular.dev/guide/signals/effect
- Angular signals guides: https://angular.dev/guide/signals, https://angular.dev/guide/signals/linked-signal, https://angular.dev/guide/signals/resource
- Angular changelog entries: 17.0.0 (signal APIs stable; `mutate` removed), 19.0.0 (effect timing; signal writes allowed; `linkedSignal`/`resource` experimental; signal inputs, outputs and queries stable), 20.0.0 (`linkedSignal`, `toSignal`, `toObservable` stable), 22.0.0 (resource stable; param statuses; SSR resource caching), 22.1.0 (`linkedSignal` `set` option): https://github.com/angular/angular/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md
- TC39 Signals proposal README (Stage 1, read 2026-10-03): https://github.com/tc39/proposal-signals

## Module 09 · Networking, storage and browser security

Specifications and documentation (read 2026-10-06; the RFC and Fetch Standard text was read in full from the cited pages):
- RFC 9110, HTTP Semantics (§9.2 safe, idempotent and cacheable methods; §15.4 redirects, the 301/302 "historical reasons" note, 307/308; §15.5 and §15.6 status codes; §10.2.3 `Retry-After`; §5.1 field names; §3.3 stateless): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html (sections linked as https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-common-method-properties and https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-connections-clients-and-ser)
- RFC 5789, PATCH ("PATCH is neither safe nor idempotent"): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5789.html
- RFC 6585, additional status codes (429 Too Many Requests): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6585.html
- Fetch Standard, HTTP-redirect fetch (a 301/302 `POST` and a 303 that is not `GET`/`HEAD` become a bodiless `GET`): https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-redirect-fetch
- MDN, Baseline compatibility (newly available, widely available): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/Baseline/Compatibility
- jsdom (the labs' DOM for cookies and Web Storage, 30.1.1): https://github.com/jsdom/jsdom
- MDN, `fetch()` (the promise "only rejects when the request fails"; it "does not reject if the server responds with HTTP status codes that indicate errors"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/fetch
- MDN, `RequestInit` (`duplex` "must have the value half"; `keepalive` body "limited to 64 kibibytes"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/RequestInit
- MDN, Using the Fetch API (bodies are `ReadableStream`s; `credentials` `omit`/`same-origin`/`include`, `SameSite` cookies still not sent cross-site): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Fetch_API/Using_Fetch
- MDN, `XMLHttpRequestUpload` (upload progress events): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/XMLHttpRequestUpload
- MDN, `XMLHttpRequest` (`withCredentials`, `abort()`, `timeout`; used by Q09.04): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/XMLHttpRequest
- RFC 9110 for the question bank (§9.2.2 "MAY automatically retry" an idempotent request, §9.2.3 "This specification defines caching semantics for GET, HEAD, and POST, although the overwhelming majority of cache implementations only support GET and HEAD", §15.5.2 `401`, §15.5.4 `403`, §15.4.4 `303`; read 2026-10-06): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html
- MDN, `Navigator.sendBeacon()` (asynchronous `POST`, queued data limited to 64 KiB, send on `visibilitychange`, avoid `unload`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Navigator/sendBeacon
- Chrome for Developers, streaming requests with `fetch` (`duplex: 'half'`; rejected over HTTP/1.x): https://developer.chrome.com/docs/capabilities/web-apis/fetch-streaming-requests
- Fetch Standard, §5 Fetch API ("currently lacking when it comes to request progression"; `keepalive` requests fail when "the sum of contentLength and inflightKeepaliveBytes is greater than 64 kibibytes", i.e. one quota shared by all in-flight keepalive requests): https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#fetch-api
- Angular 22 `HttpClient` default transport (VERSIONS.md, status table): ../VERSIONS.md
- RFC 9113, HTTP/2 (§1: pipelining "still suffers from application-layer head-of-line blocking", "TCP head-of-line blocking is not addressed by this protocol"; §3 ALPN, `h2` versus `h2c`; stateful HPACK): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9113.html and https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9113.html#section-1
- RFC 9114, HTTP/3 (§2 "one stream that is blocked or suffers packet loss does not prevent progress on other streams"; §3.1.1 `Alt-Svc: h3=":50781"`; fallback to TCP-based HTTP when QUIC fails): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9114.html
- RFC 9000, QUIC (UDP datagrams, integrated TLS handshake, 0-RTT option, connection IDs and migration): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9000.html
- MDN, connection management in HTTP/1.x (persistent connections, pipelining, "6 parallel connections", domain sharding deprecated and detrimental on HTTP/2): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/Connection_management_in_HTTP_1.x
- Chrome for Developers, removing HTTP/2 Server Push (disabled by default in Chrome 106; 103 Early Hints as the alternative): https://developer.chrome.com/blog/removing-push
- HTTP/2 FAQ ("currently no browser supports HTTP/2 unencrypted"; the page is undated, so this is the FAQ's wording): https://http2.github.io/faq/
- High Performance Browser Networking, HTTP/2 chapter (HTTP/2 removes "concatenated files, image sprites, and domain sharding"; the page is a draft-era text): https://hpbn.co/http2/
- Backend Interview Study Guide §5.2 (multiplexing): ../../Backend%20Interview%20Study%20Guide%20-%20Data%20storage%2C%20behavioral%20%26%20concurrency.md
- RFC 9111, HTTP Caching (§4.2 fresh and stale; §4.2.1 freshness lifetime from `s-maxage`, `max-age`, `Expires`; §4.2.2 heuristic freshness, "no more than some fraction", typically 10%): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111.html and https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111.html#name-freshness
- RFC 9110 validators and conditionals (§8.8.3 `ETag` strong and weak; §13.1.2 `If-None-Match` and the weak comparison; §15.4.5 `304` cannot contain content): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-if-none-match and https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-304-not-modified
- RFC 9110, read 2026-10-06 for FIX-M09-04: §8.8.2 `Last-Modified`, §8.8.2.2 (a `Last-Modified` validator is "implicitly weak" unless the server knows the representation "did not change twice during the second"), §13.1.3 `If-Modified-Since` ("MUST ignore" it when `If-None-Match` is present), §15 status classes with §15.3.2 `201`, §15.3.5 `204`, §15.5.1 `400`, §15.5.5 `404`, §15.5.10 `409`, §15.5.21 `422`, §15.6.1 `500`, §15.6.3 `502`, §15.6.5 `504`; §8.3 `Content-Type`, §12.5.1 `Accept`, §11.6.2 `Authorization`, §10.2.2 `Location`, §10.2.3 `Retry-After`, §12.5.5 `Vary`: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-status-codes, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-content-type, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-accept, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-authorization, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-location, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-retry-after, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-vary, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-last-modified, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-if-modified-since, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-comparison, https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-field-names
- MDN, bfcache glossary (read 2026-10-06: "The back/forward cache, or bfcache" enables "instant back and forward navigation" by "storing a complete snapshot of a page"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/Bfcache
- Weichselbaum et al., full paper text (read 2026-10-06 from the PDF, §2.3.1: JSONP interfaces "wrap a JavaScript object in a callback function"; a whitelisted domain with a JSONP interface lets an attacker run code by loading it as a `<script>` with an attacker-controlled callback): https://research.google/pubs/pub45542
- RFC 9113 §4.3 and §9.2, read 2026-10-06 for HPACK (field compression) and TLS use: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9113.html
- MDN, `Cache-Control` (`no-cache` stores but must be validated before reuse "even when the cache is disconnected from the origin server"; `no-store`; `must-revalidate`; `private`; `immutable`; `stale-while-revalidate`; `s-maxage`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Cache-Control
- MDN, HTTP caching guide (heuristic caching, `Vary`, reload sends `max-age=0` and validators, force reload, cache busting with hashed URLs and `immutable`, what `no-store` loses): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/Caching
- Fetch Standard, §3.3 CORS protocol and §4.10 CORS check (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin` is `*` or the exact origin; with credentials mode `include`, `*` is rejected and `Allow-Credentials: true` is case-sensitive; the same wildcard rule for `Allow-Headers`, `Allow-Methods`, `Expose-Headers`; preflight carries no credentials, needs an ok status, `Access-Control-Max-Age` default 5 seconds with an imposed limit; the CORS check runs on the response before redirect handling): https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#http-cors-protocol
- MDN, same-origin policy (origin = protocol, port, host; cross-origin writes and embedding "typically allowed", reads "typically disallowed"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Security/Same-origin_policy
- MDN, CORS guide (simple request conditions, preflight, redirects after a preflight not supported by every browser, credentialed requests and wildcards, `Vary: Origin`, forms as the reason simple requests need no opt-in): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/CORS
- MDN, site (registrable domain; schemeful same-site): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/Site
