- Section 2: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-2-fetch.test.ts` (one-shot body and `clone()`, `Uint8Array` chunks, `duplex`, abort during a body read, `Request` defaults).
- Section 3: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-3-http-versions.test.ts` (six concurrent requests: HTTP/1.1 through `Agent({ maxSockets: 2 })` gives 2 sockets and at most 2 in flight; one h2c session carries 6 concurrent streams; counts only).
- Section 4: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-4-caching.test.ts` with `etag-handler.ts` (200, then 304 with an empty body, then 200 with a new `ETag`; `If-None-Match` weak comparison, list and `*`; content-hashed file names; Node `fetch` requests a `max-age=3600` response twice, so it has no HTTP cache).
- Section 5: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-5-cors.test.ts` (Node `fetch` reads a response with no CORS headers; a foreign-`Origin` POST still runs its side effect; allow-list, `Vary: Origin` and preflight response headers sent by the lab server; `URL.origin` comparison). Browser enforcement is not observed.
- RFC 6265bis draft-22 (default-path algorithm; `__Secure-` and `__Host-` prefix rules; `Max-Age` precedence; `HttpOnly` limits non-HTTP APIs; an Internet-Draft, intended Proposed Standard, read 2026-10-06): https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-httpbis-rfc6265bis
- MDN, `Set-Cookie` (`HttpOnly` cookies still sent with `fetch`/XHR; `SameSite` `Strict`/`Lax`/`None`; "some browsers" default to `Lax`, more permissive for two minutes after setting; host-only default; default `Path`; prefixes; `Partitioned` needs `Secure`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Set-Cookie
- web.dev, SameSite cookies explained (site definition; from Chrome 80 cookies without `SameSite` are treated as `Lax`; page last updated 2019-05-07, so it describes the change as upcoming): https://web.dev/articles/samesite-cookies-explained
- Chromium, SameSite Updates (read 2026-10-06: enforcement rolled out from the week of 17 February 2020 to a limited population, temporarily rolled back on 3 April 2020, resumed with stable Chrome 84 on 14 July 2020, enabled for Chrome 80+): https://www.chromium.org/updates/same-site/
- web.dev, Schemeful Same-Site (scheme plus registrable domain; "From Chrome 86, enable about://flags/#schemeful-same-site", page last updated 2020-11-20, no default-on date given): https://web.dev/articles/schemeful-samesite
- MDN, Partitioned cookies / CHIPS (one jar per top-level site, `Secure` required, Baseline 2025, newly available since December 2025): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Privacy/Guides/Privacy_sandbox/Partitioned_cookies
- Section 6 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-6-cookies.test.ts` (jsdom 30.1.1 with `// @vitest-environment-options` url `https://app.example.com/path/page`: script `HttpOnly` ignored, server-side `HttpOnly` hidden from `document.cookie`, foreign `Domain` rejected, parent `Domain` accepted, host-only, default path, `Secure` not sent over http, `__Host-`/`__Secure-` rules, `Max-Age=0`).
- MDN, Storage quotas and eviction criteria (read 2026-10-06: Chrome up to 60% of disk, Firefox best-effort the smaller of 10% of disk and 10 GiB, Safari about 60% in browser apps; IndexedDB, Cache API under the quota, cookies and Web Storage not; Web Storage 5 MiB per store; `QuotaExceededError`; `navigator.storage.persist()`; origin data evicted whole; Safari 7 days without interaction): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria
- MDN, Using the Web Storage API (strings only, `'[object Object]'`; `localStorage` persists and is shared across tabs, `sessionStorage` per tab; `storage` event fires in other documents): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_Storage_API/Using_the_Web_Storage_API
- MDN, IndexedDB API (asynchronous, transactional, structured clone, same-origin, indexes, available in workers): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/IndexedDB_API
- MDN, `CacheStorage` (`Request`/`Response` pairs in named caches; secure contexts only; usable from workers and windows): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/CacheStorage
- Section 7 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-7-storage.test.ts` (jsdom 30.1.1 and Node 24: string coercion, JSON round trip losses versus `structuredClone`, `sessionStorage` separate, jsdom's `QuotaExceededError` with 1 MB writes; not a browser's quota).
- web.dev, Mitigate XSS with a strict CSP (allowlist policies "can be bypassed"; nonce policy `script-src 'nonce-{RANDOM}' 'strict-dynamic'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none';`; nonce "ideally 128+ bits", base64, new for every response; hash variant; `strict-dynamic` makes browsers ignore `'unsafe-inline'` and host allowlists; deploy with `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` first): https://web.dev/articles/strict-csp
- Weichselbaum et al., "CSP Is Dead, Long Live CSP" (94.68% of script-limiting policies ineffective; 14 of the 15 most allowlisted domains contain unsafe endpoints such as JSONP and hosted libraries; introduces `strict-dynamic`): https://research.google/pubs/pub45542
- MDN, Content Security Policy guide (header and `<meta>` delivery, report-only not available in `<meta>`, `report-to`, nonce must change per response so static HTML cannot carry one, hash guidance, `unsafe-inline` ignored when a nonce or hash is present, `strict-dynamic` trade-off, `frame-ancestors`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/CSP
- W3C, Content Security Policy Level 3 (§3.3 the `<meta>` element does not support `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`, `report-uri`, `frame-ancestors`, `sandbox`; §8.2 `'strict-dynamic'`): https://www.w3.org/TR/CSP3/
- MDN, Trusted Types API ("Baseline 2026, newly available, since February 2026"; sinks such as `innerHTML`; `require-trusted-types-for`, `trusted-types`, `createPolicy`; the API supplies no policy): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Trusted_Types_API
- Section 8 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-8-csp.test.ts` with `csp.ts` (Web Crypto: nonce is 16 random bytes base64 and unique over 50 calls, strict policy string, hash source of the empty string equals the known SHA-256 vector, one extra space changes the hash). CSP and Trusted Types enforcement is not observed.
- MDN, Using Service Workers (lifecycle install, waiting, activate; default scope is the script's directory and `Service-Worker-Allowed`; HTTPS required, `localhost` allowed; `fetch` event and `respondWith()`; versioned cache cleanup in `activate`; byte-different update): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Service_Worker_API/Using_Service_Workers
- web.dev, The service worker lifecycle (waiting: "delays activating until the existing service worker is no longer controlling clients"; `skipWaiting()`; `clients.claim()`; updates by navigation or functional events, 24-hour limit, "byte-different"; redundant state): https://web.dev/articles/service-worker-lifecycle
- MDN, Caching strategies for PWAs (cache first, stale-while-revalidate, network first, network only and when each suits; precaching and app shell definitions): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Progressive_web_apps/Guides/Caching
- MDN, Web application manifest (a JSON file; members `name`, `short_name`, `start_url`, `display`, `icons`, `theme_color`, `id`; `<link rel="manifest">`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Progressive_web_apps/Manifest
- MDN, Making PWAs installable (Chromium: `name` or `short_name`, 192 and 512 px `icons`, `start_url`, `display`, HTTPS; a service worker is not required): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Progressive_web_apps/Guides/Making_PWAs_installable
- Section 9 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/section-9-sw-strategies.test.ts` with `sw-strategies.ts` (cache first, network first and stale-while-revalidate as functions over an injected fetcher and a `Map`). Service worker registration, lifecycle and the Cache API are documented, not run.
- Fetch Standard, CORS-preflight fetch (read 2026-10-06 from the cited page: the CORS check runs on the real request, so the preflight answer needs the credentialed origin rules; `Access-Control-Request-Method` and CORS-unsafe request-header names; methods and header names matched against the answer, header names byte-case-insensitively, safelisted methods exempt; `Authorization` is a CORS non-wildcard request-header name): https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#cors-preflight-fetch-0
- Exercises, run by tests: `cors-model.test.ts` (E09.1), `resilient-fetch.test.ts` (E09.2, local `node:http` server, Node 24 `fetch`, `AbortSignal.any` and `AbortSignal.timeout`) and `cookie-jar.test.ts` (E09.3, jsdom 30.1.1 for the shared prefix/Domain/Secure cases; SameSite not compared) in `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/`.

## Module 10 · CSS essentials

Specifications and documentation (read 2026-10-06 from the cited pages; fetched as text, anchors checked):
- CSS 2.2, Box model (§8.3.1 adjoining vertical margins collapse, three exceptions, "Horizontal margins never collapse", clearance inhibits collapsing; §8.4 padding percentages refer to the containing block's width "even for 'padding-top' and 'padding-bottom'"): https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/box.html (linked as https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/box.html#collapsing-margins and https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/box.html#padding-properties)
- CSS 2.2, Visual formatting model (§9.4.1 floats, absolute boxes, non-block block containers and `overflow` other than `visible` establish BFCs; §9.5.2 `clear` and clearance): https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/visuren.html (linked as https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/visuren.html#flow-control)
- CSS 2.2, Visual formatting model details (§10.1 containing block of a `static` or `relative` box is the content edge of the nearest block-container or formatting-context ancestor; §10.2 `width` does not apply to non-replaced inline elements; §10.3.3 block-level width equation, `auto` fills): https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/visudet.html (linked as https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/visudet.html#containing-block-details, https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/visudet.html#blockwidth and https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/visudet.html#the-width-property)
- CSS 2.2, Conformance (§3 definition of "replaced element"): https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/conform.html#defs
- CSS Display Module Level 3 (outer and inner display type; `flow-root` "always establishes a new block formatting context"; `inline-block` is `inline flow-root`): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-display-3/
- Selectors Level 4 §5.2 (the universal selector "represents an element of any element type"; pseudo-elements are separate): https://www.w3.org/TR/selectors-4/#the-universal-selector
- MDN, `box-sizing` ("Widely available" since July 2015; `content-box` initial, `width: 350px; border: 10px solid black` renders 370px; `border-box`; not inherited): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/box-sizing
- MDN, Mastering margin collapsing (three cases, largest positive plus most negative, vertical only, never for floats and absolutely positioned elements, not in flex or grid containers): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_box_model/Mastering_margin_collapsing
- MDN, Block formatting context (the list of what creates one; contains internal floats, excludes external floats, suppresses margin collapsing; `flow-root` versus `overflow`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_display/Block_formatting_context
- MDN, Baseline (compatibility) (definitions of widely and newly available, 2.5 years, the Baseline browsers): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/Baseline/Compatibility
- jsdom (the DOM implementation behind the lab's jsdom claims): https://github.com/jsdom/jsdom
- Section 1 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/10-css-essentials/section-1-jsdom-limits.test.ts` with `support/dom.ts` (jsdom 30.1.1: no layout, `box-sizing` read as declared and not inherited, no `@layer`, `@media`, `@container`, nesting, `var()` resolution, logical-property mapping, `@scope` or `@property`; those are pins so a jsdom upgrade is noticed, not statements about browsers).
- CSS Cascading and Inheritance Level 5 (read 2026-10-06 in full from the cited page: §4.4 computed value is what inheritance transfers; §6.1 sorting order: origin and importance, context, element-attached styles, layers, specificity, order of appearance; §6.3 `!important` inverts origin order; §6.4 layers, unlayered rules in an implicit final layer, reversed for important, nesting, anonymous layers, `@import ... layer()`, `@layer` statement allowed before `@import`; §7.3 `initial`, `inherit`, `unset`, `revert`, `revert-layer`): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/ (linked as https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#cascade-sort, https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#importance, https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#layering, https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#computed and https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#defaulting-keywords)
- Selectors Level 4 §15 (specificity A-B-C; `*` ignored; `:is()`, `:not()`, `:has()` take the most specific argument, `:where()` zero; columns compared in order) and §2 pseudo-elements: https://www.w3.org/TR/selectors-4/#specificity-rules
- MDN, `@layer` ("Widely available", across browsers since March 2022; unlayered styles override layered ones, important order inverted): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@layer
- MDN, `:has()` ("Widely available", across browsers since December 2023; compatibility data read 2026-10-07 from mdn/browser-compat-data `css/selectors/has.json` and `browsers/*.json`: Safari 15.4 (2022-03-14), Chrome 105 (2022-09-02), Firefox 121 (2023-12-19); specificity of the most specific argument like `:is()` and `:not()`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/:has
- MDN, `:where()` (always zero specificity, unlike `:is()`; "Widely available" since January 2021): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/:where
- MDN, `@scope` ("Baseline 2026", newly available since March 2026; `:scope` adds class-level specificity; re-read 2026-10-07: "adds a new criterion to the CSS cascade: scoping proximity", the fewest hops to the scope root wins, and it "overrules source order but is itself overridden by … importance, layers, and specificity"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@scope
- Angular, Styling components (emulated encapsulation "generates a unique HTML attribute for each component instance ... and inserts that attribute into the CSS selectors"; read 2026-10-06): https://angular.dev/guide/components/styling
- Section 2 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/10-css-essentials/section-2-cascade.test.ts` (jsdom 30.1.1: id over class over type, order on ties, inline over id, `!important` over inline, `:where`, `:is`, `:not` and `:has` specificity, inheritance and the `inherit`, `initial` and `unset` keywords). Layers are documented from the specification only.
- CSS Flexible Box Layout Level 1 (§4.5 automatic minimum size: `auto` initial for `min-width`, content-based minimum size for non-scrollable items from the `min-content` size, zero for scroll containers; read 2026-10-06): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-flexbox-1/ (linked as https://www.w3.org/TR/css-flexbox-1/#min-size-auto)
- CSS Grid Layout Level 2 (two-dimensional layout, "grid track is a generic term for a grid column or grid row", §7.5 explicit and implicit grid, `grid-auto-rows` and `-columns`, subgrid added in Level 2; read 2026-10-06): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-grid-2/ (linked as https://www.w3.org/TR/css-grid-2/#implicit-grids)
- MDN, Basic concepts of flexbox (one-dimensional model; main and cross axis; `justify-content` on the main axis, `align-items` on the cross axis with default `stretch`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_flexible_box_layout/Basic_concepts_of_flexbox
- MDN, `flex` (`flex: 2` is `2 1 0%`, `flex: initial` is `0 1 auto`, `flex: auto` is `1 1 auto`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/flex
- MDN, `<flex>` / `fr` (a fraction of the leftover space in the grid container): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/flex_value
- MDN, `text-overflow` (does not force an overflow: "you have to set other CSS properties: `overflow` and `white-space`", for example `overflow: hidden; white-space: nowrap;`; read 2026-10-07): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/text-overflow
- MDN, `repeat()` (`auto-fill` repeats to the largest count that does not overflow; `auto-fit` behaves as `auto-fill` except that empty repeated tracks collapse after placement): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/repeat
- MDN, `gap` (gutters only between tracks, none before the first or after the last): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/gap
- MDN, Subgrid ("Widely available", across browsers since September 2023; compatibility data read 2026-10-07 from mdn/browser-compat-data `css/properties/grid-template-columns.json` (`subgrid`) and `browsers/*.json`: Firefox 71 (2019-12-10), Safari 16 (2022-09-12), Chrome 117 (2023-09-12), Edge 117 (2023-09-15); nested grids are independent unless `subgrid`; gaps inherited and overridable; line numbers restart): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_grid_layout/Subgrid
- Section 3 has no lab test: layout is documented, never observed (jsdom computes none).
- MDN, Stacking context (read 2026-10-06: the list of what creates one, including `container-type`, flex and grid items with `z-index`, `contain: layout|paint`, top-layer elements and animated `@keyframes` with `forwards`; child `z-index` "only [has] meaning within [the] parent"; elements that create none are assimilated by the parent context): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_positioned_layout/Understanding_z-index/Stacking_context
- MDN, `position` (positioned means not `static`; `static` ignores offsets and `z-index`; `fixed` relative to the viewport unless an ancestor has `transform`, `perspective` or `filter`; `sticky` sticks to the nearest ancestor with a scrolling mechanism and needs an inset): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/position
- CSS Transforms Level 1 (a `transform` other than `none` makes the element the containing block for all descendants, including fixed-position ones): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-transforms-1/#containing-block-for-all-descendants
- CSS 2.2 Appendix E, Elaborate description of stacking contexts (E.2 painting order, from the context element's background to positive `z-index` contexts): https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/zindex.html#painting-order
- MDN, Top layer (glossary: spans the viewport above all other layers; fullscreen, modal `<dialog>` via `showModal()`, popovers via `showPopover()`; each gets a stacking context; cannot be manipulated directly): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Glossary/Top_layer
- MDN, `isolation` ("Widely available", since January 2020; determines whether an element must create a new stacking context): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/isolation
- Section 4 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/10-css-essentials/section-4-stacking.test.ts` with `stacking-context.ts` (jsdom 30.1.1 reads declared values: positioned with and without `z-index`, `fixed`, `sticky`, `opacity`, `transform`, `filter`, `isolation`, `will-change`, `contain`, `container-type`, flex and grid items, the root, the nearest ancestor). Painting order is the specification's account, not observed.
- MDN, `<meta name="viewport">` (`width=device-width`, the device width in CSS pixels; establishes the value of `vw`; read 2026-10-06): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/meta/name/viewport
- MDN, Using media queries (media features, `min-`/`max-` prefixes, range syntax `(width >= 30em)`; no Baseline line on the page, so none is given for range syntax): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_media_queries/Using_media_queries
- MDN, `@media` ("Widely available", across browsers since July 2015): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@media
- MDN, CSS container queries (`container-type` `size`, `inline-size`, `normal`; inline-size applies layout, style and inline-size containment; `container-name`; container query units `cqw`, `cqi`; style queries exist): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_containment/Container_queries
- MDN, `@container` ("Widely available", across browsers since February 2023; queries the nearest ancestor query container): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@container
- CSS Containment Level 3 §3.1 (inline-size containment: inline-axis intrinsic sizes determined as if the element had no content, while content still affects the block-axis sizes; the inline size can still depend indirectly on content through block size) and `container-type` (re-read 2026-10-07: `size` "applies layout containment, style containment, and size containment", `inline-size` "applies layout containment, style containment, and inline-size containment"): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-contain-3/#containment-inline-size
- MDN, `<length>` (`em`, `rem` "A common browser default is 16px, but user-defined preferences may modify this", `ch`, small, large and dynamic viewport units, dynamic units "not stable"): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/length
- MDN, `clamp()` ("Widely available", across browsers since July 2020; `clamp(MIN, VAL, MAX)` is resolved as `max(MIN, min(VAL, MAX))`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/clamp
- MDN, CSS logical properties and values (block and inline dimensions relative to writing mode; flow-relative values `block-start`, `inline-start`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_logical_properties_and_values
- WCAG 2.2, Understanding SC 1.4.4 Resize Text ("text can be resized without assistive technology up to 200 percent without loss of content or functionality"): https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/resize-text.html
- WCAG 2.2, Failure F94 (text sized with viewport units "cannot be resized by zooming or adjusting text-size"; a likely failure of 1.4.4 when the primary method): https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Techniques/failures/F94
- Section 5 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/10-css-essentials/section-5-units.test.ts` (jsdom 30.1.1: `em` compounds and `rem` follows the root, `%` relative to the parent; the fluid `clamp()` slope and intercept arithmetic). Media and container queries are documented, never observed.
- CSS Custom Properties for Cascading Variables Level 1 (read 2026-10-06: §2 syntax, "a bare stream of CSS tokens", case-sensitive, inherited, initial value is the guaranteed-invalid value, `--foo: ;` is a valid empty value; §2.3 dependency cycles invalid at computed-value time; §3 `var()` substitution and fallback (everything after the first comma); §3.1 invalid at computed-value time computes as `unset`, the `background-color: var(--not-a-color)` example and why variables cannot fail early): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-variables-1/ (linked as https://www.w3.org/TR/css-variables-1/#custom-property, https://www.w3.org/TR/css-variables-1/#invalid-variables and https://www.w3.org/TR/css-variables-1/#cycles)
- CSS Cascade 5 §7.2 (inheritance "operates on the flattened element tree"): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade-5/#inheriting
- MDN, `@property` ("Baseline 2024", newly available since July 2024; `syntax`, `inherits`, `initial-value`; `registerProperty()` is equivalent): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@property
- MDN, `color-scheme` ("Widely available", since January 2022; changes canvas, scrollbars, default form-control colours; authors use `prefers-color-scheme` for the rest): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color-scheme
- MDN, `light-dark()` ("Baseline 2024", newly available since May 2024; needs `color-scheme: light dark`): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/color_value/light-dark
- MDN, `prefers-color-scheme` ("Widely available", since January 2020): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/@media/prefers-color-scheme
- MDN, `<meta name="color-scheme">` ("Widely available", since January 2022; document-level preference order): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/meta/name/color-scheme
- Section 6 observed by running: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/10-css-essentials/section-6-custom-properties.test.ts` (jsdom 30.1.1: inheritance and redefinition on a subtree, `setProperty` read back, undeclared names read as the empty string, names case-sensitive, `color-scheme` read as declared). `var()` resolution is documented, not observed.
- MDN, Using CSS nesting (read 2026-10-06: `&` optional for child selectors, required for compound selectors and pseudo-classes, "the specificity of the `&` nesting selector is similar to the `:is()` function"; "Concatenation (is not possible)": a nested `&__child` is treated as a type selector, and `&Element` is invalid; nested `@media`; differs from preprocessors because the browser parses it): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Guides/Nesting/Using
- MDN, Nesting selector `&` ("Widely available", across browsers since December 2023): https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Selectors/Nesting_selector
- CSS Nesting Module (§3.3 any at-rule whose body contains style rules can be nested; nested relative selectors include the specificity of their implied nesting selector): https://www.w3.org/TR/css-nesting-1/#conditionals
- Sass, `@use` ("loads mixins, functions, and variables from other Sass stylesheets", modules; Dart Sass since 1.23.0): https://sass-lang.com/documentation/at-rules/use/
- Sass, `@import` deprecation (deprecated as of Dart Sass 1.80.0, removal in 3.0.0, global built-in functions deprecated with it): https://sass-lang.com/documentation/breaking-changes/import/
- Angular, workspace configuration (`inlineStyleLanguage` accepts `css`, `less`, `sass` or `scss`, default `css`; `stylePreprocessorOptions` option-value pairs for preprocessors, including Sass `includePaths`; read 2026-10-07): https://angular.dev/reference/configs/workspace-config

## Module 12 · How Angular works

## Module 16 · Dependency injection

- angular.dev, Dependency injection in Angular (read 2026-10-08 from `adev/src/content/guide/di/overview.md` at tag `v22.2.1`: a dependency is "any object, value, function, or service that a class requires to work but does not create itself"; `@Service` "is an ergonomic shorthand for `@Injectable({providedIn: 'root'})`"; `inject()` in a constructor or a field initializer) (section 1): https://angular.dev/guide/di
- angular.dev, Hierarchical injectors (read 2026-10-08 from `adev/src/content/guide/di/hierarchical-dependency-injection.md` at tag `v22.2.1`: the `EnvironmentInjector` and `ElementInjector` hierarchies; an `ElementInjector` is "Created implicitly at each DOM element") (section 1): https://angular.dev/guide/di/hierarchical-dependency-injection
- angular.dev, Migration to the `inject` function (read 2026-10-08 from `adev/src/content/reference/migrations/inject-function.md` at tag `v22.2.1`: `ng generate @angular/core:inject`; "more accurate types and better compatibility with standard decorators") (section 1): https://angular.dev/reference/migrations/inject-function
- Spring Framework reference, Introduction to the Spring IoC Container and Beans (read 2026-10-08, Framework 7.0.9: the "inverse (hence the name, Inversion of Control)" of a bean controlling the instantiation of its dependencies; no Spring code is run) (section 1): https://docs.spring.io/spring-framework/reference/core/beans/introduction.html
- Spring Framework reference, Dependency Injection (read 2026-10-08, Framework 7.0.9: "The Spring team generally advocates constructor injection, as it lets you implement application components as immutable objects and ensures that required dependencies are not null") (section 1): https://docs.spring.io/spring-framework/reference/core/beans/dependencies/factory-collaborators.html
- `@angular/core@22.2.1`, `types/core.d.ts` (`inject` overloads, `Inject`, `Optional`, `Injectable`, `ServiceDecorator` and `Service`, all `@publicApi`, with no `@experimental` or `@developerPreview` tag; the only `@deprecated` tag on `Injectable` is on the `providedIn: NgModule` / `'any'` signature) and `types/_chrome_dev_tools_performance-chunk.d.ts` (`ProviderToken<T>` = `Type<T> | AbstractType<T> | InjectionToken<T>`), `schematics/collection.json` (`inject-migration`, alias `inject`), read 2026-10-08 (section 1)

- `@angular/core@22.2.1`, `types/_debug_node-chunk.d.ts` (`interface NgModule`: selectors available to a template "include those declared here, and those that are exported from imported NgModules"; "Declarables must belong to exactly one module"; "Exported declarations are the module's public API"; the bootstrapping module "uses the root injector"; "A lazy-loaded module has its own injector, typically a child of the app root injector"; no `@deprecated` on `NgModule` or `NgModuleDecorator`), read 2026-10-08 (section 1).

## Module 21 · Change detection

- `@angular/core@22.2.1` (`npm pack`, read 2026-10-09), `fesm2022/_debug_node-chunk.mjs` (`ɵɵtextInterpolate1`, `interpolation1`, `bindingUpdated`, `textBindingInternal`, `updateTextNode`) and `types/core.d.ts` (`provideZonelessChangeDetection`, `@publicApi 20.2`, "Zoneless is enabled by default in Angular v21+"), and `types/_debug_node-chunk.d.ts` ("OnPush is enabled by default"), for section 1.
- `@angular/core` CHANGELOG (https://github.com/angular/angular/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md, read 2026-10-09): 20.2.0 "Promote zoneless to stable", 21.0.0 "Angular no longer provides a change detection scheduler for ZoneJS-based change detection by default", 22.0.0 "Component with undefined `changeDetection` property are now `OnPush` by default", the basis of the markers of section 1.
- angular.dev, Angular without ZoneJS (`adev/src/content/guide/zoneless.md` at tag `v22.2.1`, read 2026-10-09 from `raw.githubusercontent.com/angular/angular`): "ZoneJS does not have any insight into whether application state actually changed" and "ZoneJS works by patching browser APIs", documented, for section 1.

- QB-M22-01 re-reads, 2026-10-09: WICG `Observable` explainer (https://github.com/WICG/observable/blob/master/README.md: `EventTarget.when`, `subscribe(observer, { signal })`, `addTeardown()`, the operator list `catch`, `takeUntil`, `finally`, `map`, `filter`, `take`, `drop`, `flatMap`, `reduce`, `toArray`, `forEach`, `some`, `every`, `find`, no `debounceTime` or `combineLatest`); specification draft (https://wicg.github.io/observable/, "21 November 2025", "not on the W3C Standards Track"); `mdn/browser-compat-data` `api/Observable.json` (Chrome 135, Firefox and Safari `false`); webstatus.dev API (https://api.webstatus.dev/v1/features/observable: Chrome and Edge 135, baseline `limited`, Mozilla `negative`, Apple `support`); chromestatus API (https://chromestatus.com/api/v0/features/5154593776599040: status "In development", desktop 135); RxJS `7.x` `docs_app/content/guide/observable.md` ("analogous to calling a Function"); `rxjs@7.8.2` `dist/esm/internal/Observable.js` (constructor, `subscribe`, `_trySubscribe`), `Subscriber.js` and `Subscription.js` (class chain, `closed`).
- QB-M22-02 reads, 2026-10-09: `rxjs@7.8.2` `dist/esm/internal/Subscription.js` (`add`: a teardown added to a closed subscription runs at once, `undefined` is ignored), `Observable.js` (`subscribe` passes the producer's return to `subscriber.add`, `_trySubscribe`), `Subscriber.js` and `util/reportUnhandledError.js` (`ConsumerObserver`, the timeout that calls `config.onUnhandledError` or throws); MDN, `EventTarget.removeEventListener` (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/EventTarget/removeEventListener: a call that identifies no registered listener "has no effect", read 2026-10-09); `labs/angular/src/app/modules/22-rxjs-foundations/outputs/q22-outputs.spec.ts` (tests `Q22.06`, `Q22.07`, `Q22.08`, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`; Q22.07 backs a Bug hunt, not an Output answer) and `exercise-1-mini-observable/mini-observable.ts` (Excerpt in Q22.09).
- QB-M22-03 reads, 2026-10-09: `rxjs@7.8.2` `dist/esm/internal/operators/share.js` (the Subject joined before the source connects, the reference count, `handleReset` with `resetOnRefCountZero` and `resetOnComplete`), `observable/connectable.js` (`connect` runs `defer(() => source).subscribe(subject)`), `Subject.js` (`class Subject extends Observable`, `next`, `_subscribe`, `_innerSubscribe`), and the `@deprecated Will be removed in v8` tags in `dist/types/internal/operators/multicast.d.ts`, `publish.d.ts`, `publishReplay.d.ts`, `refCount.d.ts` and `observable/ConnectableObservable.d.ts`; RxJS documentation, `7.x` branch, `docs_app/content/deprecations/multicasting.md` (the refactor map: `ConnectableObservable` and `multicast` with a factory to `connectable`, with `refCount` and `publish().refCount()` to `share`, a selector to `connect`, `publishBehavior` with a `BehaviorSubject`); Project Reactor reference sources at tag `v3.8.7` (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/reactor/reactor-core/v3.8.7/docs/modules/ROOT/pages/reactiveProgramming.adoc, `advancedFeatures/reactor-hotCold.adoc`, `coreFeatures/mono.adoc`: "starts anew for each `Subscriber`", `just` and `defer`, `share()` and `replay(...)`, "at most `n` elements", "push-pull hybrid", "emits at most one item"; the rendered pages at projectreactor.io answered HTTP 401 at the time of this task and the Javadoc was not re-read, documented, not run); `labs/angular/src/app/modules/22-rxjs-foundations/outputs/q22-outputs.spec.ts` (tests `Q22.11` and `Q22.12`, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`, jsdom `<button>` and fake timers).
- QB-M22-04 reads, 2026-10-09: `rxjs@7.8.2` `dist/esm/internal/BehaviorSubject.js` (`_subscribe`, `getValue`, `next` stores `_value` before `Subject.next`), `AsyncSubject.js` (`next`, `complete`, `_checkFinalizedStatuses`), `ReplaySubject.js` (`_subscribe`, `_trimBuffer`), `Subject.js` (`next`, `complete`, `unsubscribe`, `_throwIfClosed`, `_innerSubscribe`), `util/pipe.js` (`pipeFromArray`); RxJS documentation, `7.x` branch, `docs_app/content/guide/importing.md` ("`'rxjs/operators'` export site has been deprecated", the replacement table) and `guide/operators.md` ("a pure operation: the previous Observable stays unmodified"); `labs/angular/src/app/modules/22-rxjs-foundations/outputs/q22-outputs.spec.ts` (tests `Q22.16` and `Q22.17`, and `Q22.18`, which backs a Bug hunt, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`).
- QB-M22-05 reads, 2026-10-09: `rxjs@7.8.2` `dist/esm/internal/operators/first.js` and `last.js` (`take(1)` or `takeLast(1)` then `throwIfEmpty` or, when a second argument is passed, `defaultIfEmpty`), `single.js` (`SequenceError` at the second match, `NotFoundError` or `EmptyError` at complete), `scanInternals.js`, `scan.js` and `reduce.js` (`arguments.length >= 2` as the seed test, `emitOnNext`, `emitBeforeComplete`), `tap.js` (forwards `error` and `complete` through `createOperatorSubscriber`), `observable/defer.js` (the factory runs inside the subscribe function); `labs/angular/src/app/modules/22-rxjs-foundations/outputs/q22-outputs.spec.ts` (tests `Q22.24` and `Q22.25`, and `Q22.21` and `Q22.22`, which back a Design and a Bug-hunt answer, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`).

## Module 23 · RxJS in depth

- `rxjs@7.8.2` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/rxjs`, read 2026-10-09), `dist/esm/internal/operators/mergeInternals.js` (`mergeInternals`: `buffer`, `active`, `isComplete`, `outerNext`, `checkComplete`), `mergeMap.js` (`concurrent = Infinity`), `concatMap.js` (`mergeMap(project, 1)`), `switchMap.js` (`innerSubscriber?.unsubscribe()` before the next inner, `checkComplete` in the inner's complete callback), `exhaustMap.js` (the `innerSub` guard), `switchAll.js`, `mergeAll.js`, `concatAll.js`, `exhaustAll.js`, `flatMap.js` (`flatMap = mergeMap`), `exhaust.js` (`exhaust = exhaustAll`), `OperatorSubscriber.js` (`createOperatorSubscriber`; `onFinalize` runs after `super.unsubscribe()`), `dist/types/internal/types.d.ts` (the `ObservableInput` union), `dist/types/internal/operators/{switchMap,mergeMap,concatMap,exhaustMap,flatMap,exhaust}.d.ts` (the `@deprecated` tags of result selectors, `flatMap` and `exhaust`) and `CHANGELOG.md` (7.0.0-beta.11 "exhaustAll: renamed `exhaust` to `exhaustAll`"), for section 1.
- `rxjs@6.0.0` (`npm pack`, read 2026-10-09), `internal/operators/switchMap.d.ts`: "@deprecated resultSelector is no longer supported, use inner map instead", the basis of the result-selector marker of section 1.
- Section 1 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/concepts/section-1-flattening.spec.ts` (Excerpt; tested by the nine `Section 1:` tests, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`) and `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/legacy/deprecated-forms.ts` (the section 1 legacy functions, run by two of those tests, compiled by `ng build`).
- `rxjs@7.8.2` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/rxjs`, read 2026-10-09), `dist/esm/internal/operators/expand.js` (`mergeInternals` with the `expand` flag), `mergeScan.js`, `switchScan.js` (the state is updated only when an inner emits) and `CHANGELOG.md` (7.0.0-beta.8 "switchScan: add switchScan() operator"), for section 2.
- `@angular/common@22.2.1` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/@angular/common`, read 2026-10-09), `fesm2022/_module-chunk.mjs` (`FetchBackend.handle`: `new AbortController()`, `signal` passed to `doRequest`, teardown `aborter.abort()` when not `done`; `HttpXhrBackend.handle`: teardown `xhr.abort()` when `readyState !== DONE`) and `fesm2022/http-testing.mjs` (`HttpClientTestingBackend.handle`: teardown sets `_cancelled = true`; `TestRequest.cancelled`), for section 2's cancellation paragraph and Framework vs platform note.
- Section 2 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/concepts/section-2-choosing.spec.ts` (Excerpt; tested by the seven `Section 2:` tests, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`; `TestBed` with `provideHttpClient()` and `provideHttpClientTesting()`).
- `rxjs@7.8.2` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/rxjs`, read 2026-10-09), `dist/esm/internal/operators/catchError.js` (`caught` is `catchError(selector)(source)`; the source subscription is unsubscribed before the replacement subscribes, `syncUnsub`), `finalize.js` (`source.subscribe(subscriber)`, then `subscriber.add(callback)` in `finally`), `dist/types/internal/observable/throwError.d.ts` (the `@deprecated` value form: "will be removed in v8", "a more appropriate stack trace"), `dist/types/internal/operators/onErrorResumeNextWith.d.ts` and `CHANGELOG.md` (7.0.0-beta.1 "finalize: callback will be called after the source observable is torn down"; 7.0.0-beta.5 "throwError: now accepts a factory to create the error"; 7.6.0 "onErrorResumeNextWith: renamed `onErrorResumeNext`"; 5.5.0-beta.0 "catchError: add higher-order lettable version of `catch`" and "finalize: add higher-order lettable version of finally"); a `grep` of `dist` finds no `_catch` or `_finally`, for section 3.
- `rxjs@6.6.7` (`npm pack`, read 2026-10-09), `internal/observable/throwError.d.ts`: a single `throwError(error, scheduler?)` signature, no factory overload and no `@deprecated` tag.
- `rxjs@5.5.12` (`npm pack`, read 2026-10-09), `add/operator/catch.d.ts` and `add/operator/finally.d.ts` (they add `catch`/`_catch` and `finally`/`_finally` to the `Observable` interface), `operators/catchError.d.ts`, `operators/finalize.d.ts`, for section 3's legacy history.
- Section 3 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/concepts/section-3-errors.spec.ts` (Excerpt; tested by the nine `Section 3:` tests, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`) and `legacy/deprecated-forms.ts` (`legacyThrowErrorValue`).
- `rxjs@7.8.2` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/rxjs`, read 2026-10-09), `dist/esm/internal/operators/retry.js` (`retry`: `count = Infinity`, a number `delay` becomes `timer(delay)`, a function goes through `innerFrom`, the notifier's complete calls `subscriber.complete()`, `resetOnSuccess` zeroes `soFar` on `next`), `repeat.js` (`repeat`: re-subscribes on complete, a number or function `delay`), `retryWhen.js` (the notifier's subscriber has no complete handler, so its complete reaches the result), `OperatorSubscriber.js` (`_error` wraps the error callback in `try` and forwards a throw to `destination.error`); `dist/types/internal/operators/retry.d.ts` (`RetryConfig`), `repeat.d.ts` (`RepeatConfig`), `retryWhen.d.ts` and `repeatWhen.d.ts` ("@deprecated Will be removed in v9 or v10"); `CHANGELOG.md` 7.0.0-beta.0 ("retry: add config to reset error count on successful emission"), 7.3.0 ("retry: Now supports configurable delay as a named argument"), 7.5.0 ("repeat: now has configurable delay").
- `rxjs@7.5.4` and `rxjs@7.5.5` (`npm pack`, read 2026-10-09; also 7.5.0-7.5.3, 7.5.6, 7.5.7 and 7.6.0), `dist/types/internal/operators/retryWhen.d.ts` and `repeatWhen.d.ts`: no `@deprecated` tag up to 7.5.4, the tag from 7.5.5 on; the basis of the `retryWhen`/`repeatWhen` markers of section 4.
- Section 4 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/concepts/section-4-retry.spec.ts` (Excerpt; tested by the eight `Section 4:` tests, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`; `TestBed` with `provideHttpClient()` and `provideHttpClientTesting()` for the HTTP retry) and `legacy/deprecated-forms.ts` (`legacyRetryWhen`, `legacyRepeatWhen`).
- `rxjs@7.8.2` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/rxjs`, read 2026-10-09), `dist/esm/internal/operators/shareReplay.js` (`shareReplay` returns `share({ connector: () => new ReplaySubject(bufferSize, windowTime, scheduler), resetOnError: true, resetOnComplete: false, resetOnRefCountZero: refCount })`, `refCount = false`, `bufferSize = Infinity`), `share.js` (`handleReset`: `true` resets at once, `false` never, a function's Observable resets on its first value; `cancelReset` when a subscriber arrives before an error or complete), `connect.js` (`connect`: one `source.subscribe(subject)`, the selector gets `fromSubscribable(subject)`), `ReplaySubject.js` (`windowTime` timed by `dateTimestampProvider`); `dist/types/internal/operators/share.d.ts` (`ShareConfig`); `CHANGELOG.md` 6.4.0 ("shareReplay: Add configuration object for named arguments, and add argument to support unsubscribing from source"), 7.0.0-beta.10 ("share: Make `share` completely configurable", "connect: Adds new `connect` operator"), 7.1.0 ("share: use another observable to control resets").
- Section 5 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/concepts/section-5-multicasting.spec.ts` (Excerpt; tested by the seven `Section 5:` tests, run 2026-10-09 under `ng test`).
- `rxjs@7.8.2` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/rxjs`, read 2026-10-09), `dist/esm/internal/operators/takeUntil.js` (`takeUntil`: the notifier's value calls `subscriber.complete()`), `switchMap.js` (`checkComplete`: `isComplete && !innerSubscriber`), `Subject.js` (`get observed`: `observers.length > 0`); `CHANGELOG.md` 7.1.0 ("All subjects now have an `observed` property"); `rxjs@7.0.1` and `rxjs@7.1.0` (`npm pack`, read 2026-10-09), `dist/types/internal/Subject.d.ts`: no `observed` in 7.0.1, `get observed()` in 7.1.0.
- `@angular/core@22.2.1` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/@angular/core`, read 2026-10-09), `fesm2022/rxjs-interop.mjs` (`toSignal`: `cleanupRef?.onDestroy(sub.unsubscribe.bind(sub))`, unregistered on complete or error; `takeUntilDestroyed`: `source.pipe(takeUntil(destroyed$))`), `types/rxjs-interop.d.ts` (`@publicApi 19.0` for `takeUntilDestroyed`); `@angular/common@22.2.1`, `fesm2022/_common_module-chunk.mjs` (`AsyncPipe.ngOnDestroy` calls `_dispose`).

- `@angular/core@22.2.1` (installed in `labs/angular/node_modules/@angular/core`, read 2026-10-10), `fesm2022/rxjs-interop.mjs` (`toSignal`: state kinds 0, 1 and 2, `assertNotInReactiveContext`, `makeToSignalEqual`, the `RuntimeError` 601 for `requireSync`, `cleanupRef?.onDestroy`; `toObservable`: a `ReplaySubject(1)` fed by an `effect` with `manualCleanup: true`, `subject.error` on a throwing source, `subject.complete()` on `DestroyRef` destruction; `rxResource`: the `abort` listener on `params.abortSignal` that unsubscribes, `RuntimeError` -991 when the stream completes unresolved; `outputFromObservable`: `takeUntilDestroyed` and `RuntimeError` 953 after destruction; `outputToObservable`: `observer.complete()` on `getOutputDestroyRef(ref).onDestroy`; `pendingUntilEvent`: `PendingTasks.add()` removed on the first `next`, `complete`, `error` or unsubscribe), found by `grep`, for section 7.

Use scheduled`); `CHANGELOG.md` 6.5.0 (`scheduled` added, scheduled versions of `from`, `range` et al. deprecated).

- Exercise 23.1 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/exercise-1-flattening-choice/` (`flows.ts` and `flows.spec.ts`: Excerpts; the five `E23.1` tests, run 2026-10-10 under `ng test`; each runs the operator flows and the `map` plus `All` flows inside `TestScheduler.run`, and a mutation of `saveFlow`, `submitFlow` and `thumbnailFlow` made three of the five fail). The `All` equivalence rests on the section 1 read of `switchAll` and `mergeAll` in `rxjs@7.8.2`.
- Exercise 23.2 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/exercise-2-resilient-loader/` (`resilient-loader.ts` and `resilient-loader.spec.ts`: Excerpts; the six `E23.2` tests, run 2026-10-10 under `ng test` with `provideHttpClient()`, `provideHttpClientTesting()` and `vi.useFakeTimers()`; each runs `ResilientLoader` and the table-backed `TableBackoffLoader`, and a mutation that retried every error, dropped `finalize` and kept the other operators made three of the six fail). The `cancelled` flag is `HttpTestingController`'s report from `@angular/common` 22.2.1 (`TestRequest.cancelled`), not a network observation; `retry`, `catchError` and `finalize` behavior rests on the section 3 and section 4 reads of `rxjs@7.8.2`.
- Exercise 23.3 code: `labs/angular/src/app/modules/23-rxjs-in-depth/exercise-3-cache-service/` (`config-cache.ts` and `live-prices.ts`: Excerpts; `counting-source.ts` and `cache.spec.ts`: the six `E23.3` tests, run 2026-10-10 under `ng test` with `TestBed` and `vi.useFakeTimers()`; the three cache tests run `ConfigCache` and the `share`-based `ShareConfigCache`; a mutation of `LivePrices` to plain `share()` and one of the cache to `resetOnError: false` each made one test fail, and `refCount: true` on the cache made none fail, because every test keeps a subscriber). The `shareReplay` and `share` behavior rests on the section 5 reads of `shareReplay.js` and `share.js` in `rxjs@7.8.2`.

The strategies rest on the section 6 reads of `takeUntil.js` and `switchMap.js` in `rxjs@7.8.2` and of `toSignal` in `@angular/core@22.2.1`.
